- [x] **T1** Function oracle: run the original's code in tests instead of recording it (user 2026-10-08; recomp idea, ~8% of agents' tool I/O over the last 6 days went to driving PCSX2, plus the bogus "needs PCSX2" blocks). A small R5900 interpreter in a test-only crate (EE integer ops + COP1 through `hst_sim::ps2` + VU0 macro ops through `hst_sim::vu0`) that loads the code from the user's own GAME.BIN/overlay ELFs at test time (`context/elf/`, disc-ID checked like `hst_data::exe`; no retail code or addresses in git) and RAM from a dump (`context/ram/`), calls one function with chosen arguments, stops at its return, and hands back registers + touched memory. Tests then compare a port against the original on many generated inputs, bit-exact, with no PCSX2. Not for timing, frame flow or rendering: those still need recordings. Done when one existing PINE-verified port (e.g. a `libm.rs` function and one AI decision) is also checked through the oracle over ≥10k inputs, and `AGENT.md` *Cheap context* says when to use it instead of a recording → new `crates/hst-oracle` · `hst-sim/src/ps2.rs` `vu0.rs` · `hst-data/src/exe.rs` · `research/dis.sh`
- [x] **B55** Pause menu widescreen/dynamic (user 2026-10-08): lay out P19a's pause menu for any aspect/resolution like B17's team and score panels (pinned/scaled, not a stretched or centred 4:3 box); see *All UI at any aspect ratio* above → `play/menu.rs` · `play/widescreen.rs` · `play/panel.rs`
- [x] **B70** Main menu widescreen/dynamic (user 2026-10-08): lay out the main menu and every page under it (title, mode/settings, player and character select, mod select) for any aspect ratio and resolution like B17's panels (pinned/scaled, not a stretched or centred 4:3 box); `--4x3` keeps the original layout; compare against `tools/screenshot.sh` at 4:3; see *All UI at any aspect ratio* → `play/main_menu.rs` · `play/widescreen.rs`
- [x] **B82** PRIORITY #3 (user 2026-10-09) Linux release must run on SteamOS / Steam Deck: `tools/build.sh` builds the Linux binary on the dev machine (glibc 2.44), so it needs a newer glibc than SteamOS ships and won't start there (a player report). Build against an older glibc (e.g. `cargo zigbuild --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.<ver>`, the oldest glibc the deps allow, at or below SteamOS's current one: look it up, don't guess) or `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` if it works there (watch out: a static musl binary can't load the system's Vulkan/GL/udev/ALSA libraries that Bevy/wgpu open at runtime, so the older-glibc path is the likely one). - [x] **B154b** Online desync recovery by state transfer (split from B154 part 4): B154 makes a desync non-fatal (each machine logs it, writes `desync-*.json` — a download + OPFS + console line in the browser — notes it on screen and plays on), but the machines stay diverged. Make the host resync the peers: serialize the hashed state (`Game`, `Motions`, `TickPads` and the module resources in `snapshot.rs`, mapping entities through `Figure`; `snapshot::save` is closures, so it needs serde or a byte codec per resource), send it with its tick F over `net.rs`, the peers restore it and re-run their logged inputs from F (ggrs: load the cell, ignore desync events older than F; lockstep: just continue). Keep the log line + report on every desync. Test: force a divergence in `play_rollback` and check both hashes match again a few ticks after the transfer. Done when a forced desync in a two-instance match is resynced and the match finishes → `play/netplay.rs` · `play/rollback.rs` · `play/snapshot.rs` · `net.rs` | after B154

- [x] **B196** Recover when a player stops responding online (user 2026-10-13): if a peer goes silent mid-match (crash, lost network, closed tab, frozen machine) the others must not hang or desync. Detect it (no inputs/keep-alives for a few seconds), pause for everyone with a "waiting for <player>" notice and a countdown, let the player reconnect and rejoin the same match (re-handshake, resync to the confirmed state at the same tick), and if they don't come back in time, recover without them: the remaining players choose either a bot takes over that seat (switched in on the same tick on every peer, so the match stays in lockstep) or the match ends with a result screen and everyone goes back to the menu. Never an endless freeze or a silent drop to the menu. Must not touch the sim or rollback determinism for the players still in. Done when killing one peer's process mid-rally (and pulling its network) in a 2- and a 4-peer match ends in a clean reconnect, a bot takeover, or a clean end on every remaining peer, native and browser → `net.rs` · `play/netplay.rs` · `play/rollback.rs` · `play/main_menu/online.rs` | with B162
- [x] **B204** Mod store downloads stream with progress; big mods crash the browser (bug, user 2026-10-13): installing Anya (490 MB) from Browse mods in the web build sat on "Downloading Anya (490MB)..." with no progress, then crashed. Cause (from the code): `modstore::get` buffers the whole zip (then `.to_vec()` copies it), `modfs::install` unpacks it all into memory, stages it under `.import` and clones it again into `MEM`, then `hstModSave` copies it to OPFS: several full copies at once, past wasm32's 4 GB, and the unzip/checks block the only thread. Fix: stream the download (`reqwest` `bytes_stream` / `Response::chunk`) straight to OPFS on web and to a temp file on desktop, hashing sha256 as the chunks arrive; show progress on the status line (received / total MB and %, from `Content-Length` or `meta.size`), sent as progress events through `modstore::take`; unpack from the stored file one entry at a time (written out as it goes, no whole-mod copy in memory, no staging clone) and yield to the browser between entries so the page stays responsive; on a failed hash or check remove what was written. Same safety checks as today (HTTPS from the store only, sha256 before anything is unpacked, `modfs` checks). Done when Anya installs in the browser (and desktop) with a live percentage, peak memory stays well under the mod's size × 2 (browser task manager), and the `package_then_install` test still passes. Also slow (user 2026-10-13): loading installed mods in the browser build is slow (startup and the roster), measure where the time goes (OPFS reads, unzip, model/texture decode, preview bakes) and fix it as part of this → `modstore.rs` (get, install, put, keep) · `modfs.rs` (install, unpack) · `web.rs` (mod_save) · web/index.html (hstModSave, OPFS) · `play/main_menu/mods_screen.rs` (status)

- [x] **B197** One slow player slows the whole online match (bug, user 2026-10-13): when one peer lags (low frame rate, a hitch), every peer's game slows with it; it shouldn't. Find where the others wait on it (ggrs's prediction window filling because the slow peer runs fewer ticks, the time-sync "wait" recommendation, or the sim tick tied to the render frame). Fix: run the match clock in real time on every peer independent of its frame rate (a slow-rendering peer catches up by running several sim ticks per frame, drawing only the last), stop the fast peers from slowing down to match the slowest beyond a small frame-advantage correction, and keep the other peers smooth through a short hitch by predicting (rollback) instead of stalling. A peer that truly can't keep up for long falls behind on its own ("connection poor" on its screen); if it stops sending altogether, B196 handles it. Must stay deterministic: same inputs → same match on every peer. Done when, in a 2- and a 4-peer match, one peer capped to 20 fps (and one given 1 s hitches) leaves the others at full speed with no visible slowdown and no desync → `play/netplay.rs` · `play/rollback.rs` · `net.rs` · `play.rs` (fixed-step) | with B196, B162 — it's the game logic, not just frame stutter (user 2026-10-13): the match itself runs slower (ticks per real second drop), not just the drawing; that must never happen. The sim must keep its fixed 60 Hz tick rate in real time on every peer whatever one peer's frame rate is (catch up by running several ticks per frame, rollback predicts the slow peer's inputs). Test: one peer throttled to a low frame rate, the others still advance ticks at 60/s

Find how a trail that short starts (a swing begun with its motion almost over, likely a rollback/resync restoring mid-swing, or a stroke cancelled into another) and what the original does there: check the game's code for this fade (MIPS `div` by 0 doesn't trap unless the compiler added a `teq`/`break 7` check; if it doesn't trap, port its quotient/LO result exactly, the trail is gameplay-neutral but sits in the hashed state). Add a test that a 1-, 2- and 3-frame trail ticks without panicking and matches the original's alpha, and that a rollback across the start of a swing doesn't panic. Done when the test passes and no hst-sim integer division can panic on a short length → `hst-sim/src/effect.rs` (Trail::tick) · `play/rollback.rs`

- [x] **B165** Touch buttons don't register every press (bug, user 2026-10-13, priority raised: happens every time the analog stick and a button are touched at once, so start with multi-touch: per-pointer tracking, a second touch must not cancel or steal the first): in the browser build on a phone, the on-screen buttons sometimes miss a tap. - [x] **B198** Online match deadlocks, each peer waiting for the other (bug, user 2026-10-13): mid-match one screen sat on "waiting for 1P" while the other sat on "waiting for 2P", both games still running, forever. Both peers are alive, so it's a lost message nobody resends or a wait both sides enter at once: inputs/acks over unreliable datagrams with no resend, both peers at the end of the prediction window each waiting on inputs the other already sent, or a step that needs a "ready" from each side (point end, change of ends, pause/resume, a B196-style wait) where each waits before sending its own. Find which from both peers' logs (save them as B154 does) and fix it so every wait resends what the other side might be missing until it's acked and no step needs the other side to go first. Test with packet loss and delay injected (drop 10–30 % of datagrams, 150 ms RTT) through whole matches, 2 and 4 peers, native and browser. Done when those matches finish with no mutual wait and any wait clears itself within a second of the network recovering → `net.rs` · `play/netplay.rs` · `play/rollback.rs` · `handshake.rs` | with B196, B197
- [x] **B200** Ignore buttons while an online match loads, and the disconnect while loading (bug, user 2026-10-13): an online match disconnected during the loading screen while P2 was mashing buttons. Inputs pressed while loading must not reach the match, the netcode or any menu behind the loading screen (drop them until the match's first tick on every peer; held buttons count as fresh only once released and pressed again), on every peer, native and browser. Also find why the load disconnected: inputs/messages sent before the other peer finished loading, a peer's long load (B187-style bakes, disc reads) blocking keep-alives past the timeout, or a load that differs between peers. Keep-alives must keep flowing during a load and the match starts together once every peer has loaded. Done when two peers (one mashing every button through the load, one on a slow load: CPU-throttled browser) reach the first serve together with no disconnect and no stray input → `play/netplay.rs` · `net.rs` · `play/main_menu/online.rs` · `play.rs` (loading, read_input) | with B162, B196

- [x] **B275** Online: a guest left with "bad params override" when the host gave a COM a mod character (user 2026-10-13): in an online lobby, after a player joined, the host picked a mod character for a computer seat and the guest left with "left: … bad params override". That's `mods_online.rs Wire::check` refusing the pick: one of the mod's `overrides` has a key that `mods::is_column` doesn't know, or a value with a character outside digits and " ./-+" (e.g. an exponent `1e-3`, a comma, a letter, an empty value). Find which mod and override: log the refused key/value in the message. Then fix both ends: (1) `mods::read` and `check` must accept exactly the same overrides, so anything a mod loads with locally is also accepted from a peer (or the mod is refused at read time, not mid-lobby). Builds must agree on the column names (web vs native, older builds). (2) A refused pick must not throw the guest out: keep them in the lobby and give the host a message that the pick was refused and why (or put that seat on a stand-in). Done when the same mod as a COM pick is accepted by a guest, and a really bad pick only shows a message → `mods_online.rs` (Wire::check) · `mods.rs` (read, is_column) · `net.rs` (where a check failure ends the link) · `play/main_menu/online.rs` | with M2i — Then (user 2026-10-13): rejoining that lobby by its code said "Couldn't join: No open lobby has the code …" (`net.rs` connect: the DHT `resolve` of the short code found nothing) though the host's lobby was still open. Check whether the host stops publishing or removes its code record when a guest leaves or a seat is filled, or the code's DHT record (the slot) is overwritten/expires. A host whose lobby is open must stay joinable by its code until it starts or closes

- [x] **B298** Zen (Firefox/Gecko) on Windows 11 draws the character select wrong (friend's report, 2026-10-14): grid face icons come out as dark/grey blobs or stray body parts (hair, torso, racket) instead of faces, 3P's card preview is empty (just a blurred background), the ticker text is in a monospace fallback font. Likely Gecko's WebGPU path (the page picks pkg-webgpu when an adapter exists): skinning/textures in the baked previews (previews.rs, inspect.rs). Repro locally with /opt/zen-browser-bin/zen (Zen 1.23, Gecko) over WebDriver BiDi like tools/web_check.mjs (input.setFiles for the ISO); compare with ?webgl2. If WebGL2 is right and WebGPU can't be fixed, make index.html pick WebGL2 on Gecko. Files: web/index.html, crates/hst/src/play/main_menu/previews.rs, crates/hst/src/inspect.rs, tools/web_check.mjs — Same friend's match screen (Zen, Win 11) is broken too, so it's the whole renderer, not just the select: court ground mesh has missing triangles (blue clear colour shows through), characters' skinning explodes (limbs/racket strings fanned out, heads detached, umpire's chair a spike), props floating. HUD (Sets/Games banner) draws fine. Points at skinned/indexed meshes on Gecko (vertex/index buffer layout, skin uniform/storage buffers), WebGPU first suspect. — Another friend on Zen + Linux (Omarchy) sees it fine; a third screenshot from the Windows friend shows the same exploded skinning, broken court and grey HUD face icons. Firefox ships WebGPU on Windows but not by default on Linux, so Linux Zen gets the WebGL2 build: this is Gecko's WebGPU (Windows, D3D12) path. Quick fix: index.html picks WebGL2 when navigator.userAgent has 'Firefox' (one condition on the webgpu const); proper fix only if Gecko WebGPU can be made to draw right.

- [x] **B270** No reactions after a double fault (user 2026-10-13): when a point ends on a double fault, the players don't play their point reactions (the losing server's `di`/upset, the receiver's `gu`), which the original plays. Leads: `play.rs`'s Post phase only calls `react()` when `post.reacted` turns on with `post.event` set, so check whether `hst_sim::flow`'s timeline for call 3 (double fault) sets them as the original's does. Otherwise `react()` gives the server a reaction that its serve state (`served`, the toss/serve motion, `bodyhit::standing`) overrides or skips. Check against the original: record or screenshot a double fault in PCSX2 (which motions each player plays, when) and compare with `--shot`s. Done when a double fault in the port plays the same reactions as the original at the same moment, with a test on the flow/react path → `play.rs` (Post phase, react) · `hst-sim/src/flow.rs` · `hst-sim/src/motion.rs` reaction
